Actually, I think the most robust fix is to make the code handle both cases:

If the widget is defined (from base_params), use its value

If the widget is not defined, use the default value

The current code:

anos_processar = dbutils.widgets.get("anos") if dbutils.widgets.get("anos") else "2023,2024,2025"

This fails because dbutils.widgets.get("anos") throws an error if the widget doesn't exist. The fix should be:

try:

anos_processar = dbutils.widgets.get("anos")

except:

anos_processar = "2023,2024,2025"

anos = [int(ano.strip()) for ano in anos_processar.split(",")]

Or better yet, explicitly create the widget with a default value first:

dbutils.widgets.text("anos", "2023,2024,2025")

anos_processar = dbutils.widgets.get("anos")

anos = [int(ano.strip()) for ano in anos_processar.split(",")]

The second approach is more robust because it explicitly creates the widget with a default value. If the job passes a different value via base_params, the dbutils.widgets.text() call will use the passed value (or create the widget if it doesn't exist).

Wait, actually, dbutils.widgets.text() creates the widget with a default value. If the job has already created the widget with a different value via base_params, calling dbutils.widgets.text() might override it. Let me check.

Actually, in Databricks, when you call dbutils.widgets.text("anos", "2023,2024,2025"), it creates the widget with the default value. If the widget already exists (from base_params), the behavior depends on the Databricks version:

In some versions, it keeps the existing value

In some versions, it overrides with the default

The safest approach is to use try/except:

try:

anos_processar = dbutils.widgets.get("anos")

except Exception:

anos_processar = "2023,2024,2025"

anos = [int(ano.strip()) for ano in anos_processar.split(",")]

This way, if the widget is defined (from base_params), it uses the passed value. If not, it falls back to the default.

The error is interesting. The try/except is there (lines 2-10), and the dbutils.notebook.exit() call is at line 10. But the error occurs at line 12 (contagem_bronze = df_bronze.count()), which is OUTSIDE the try/except block.

Looking at the traceback:

File <command-...>, line 12, in <module>

9 print(f"⚠ Tabela bronze não encontrada: {e}")

10 dbutils.notebook.exit("SKIPPED - bronze table not found")

---> 12 contagem_bronze = df_bronze.count()

So the execution goes:

Line 2-7: try: df_bronze = spark.read.table(...).filter(...) — this creates a DataFrame but the table doesn't exist. However, Spark uses lazy evaluation! The spark.read.table() call itself might not throw an error immediately. The filter() call also doesn't throw because it's lazy.

The except block is NOT triggered because the lazy operations didn't raise an exception.

Line 12: df_bronze.count() triggers the actual execution, which then tries to resolve the table and fails with TABLE_OR_VIEW_NOT_FOUND. But this is OUTSIDE the try/except!

The problem is Spark's lazy evaluation. spark.read.table() and .filter() are lazy — they don't actually access the table until an action like .count() is called. So the try/except around the DataFrame creation doesn't catch the error because the error only occurs at .count() time.

I need to move the .count() and printSchema() INSIDE the try/except block, or restructure the code so the try/except covers the action call as well.

The fix is simple: move the entire block including count and printSchema inside the try/except.

Let me read the current cell to see the exact content and fix it.

O problema é claro: Spark usa lazy evaluation. O spark.read.table() não lança erro na criação do DataFrame — só no .count() (action). Meu try/except envolve apenas a criação, mas o erro acontece no .count() que está fora do bloco. Vou mover o count() e printSchema() para dentro do try/except.

Actually, the simplest approach that's known to work is:

Use requests to download the Parquet file to /tmp/ or /dbfs/tmp/

Then spark.read.parquet("/tmp/filename.parquet") or spark.read.parquet("dbfs:/tmp/filename.parquet")

Let me think about which approach to use for notebook 101:

Option A: Download Parquet to local file, then read with Spark

import requests

url = "https://dadosabertos.aneel.gov.br/dataset/.../interrupcoes-energia-eletrica-2024.parquet"

local_path = f"/tmp/aneel_interrupcoes_{ano}.parquet"

r = requests.get(url, timeout=300, stream=True)

with open(local_path, 'wb') as f:

for chunk in r.iter_content(chunk_size=8192):

f.write(chunk)

df_raw = spark.read.parquet(f"file://{local_path}")

Option B: Use dbutils.fs.cp to copy from HTTP to DBFS

dbutils.fs.cp(url, f"dbfs:/tmp/aneel_interrupcoes_{ano}.parquet")

df_raw = spark.read.parquet(f"dbfs:/tmp/aneel_interrupcoes_{ano}.parquet")

For notebook 101, cell 6, the new content:

# Processar cada ano disponível com verificação de idempotência

import os

for ano in anos_a_processar:

try:

print(f"\n{'='*60}")

print(f"Processando ano: {ano}")

print(f"{'='*60}")

url_arquivo = URLS_PARQUET.get(ano)

if not url_arquivo:

print(f"⚠ Sem URL mapeada para ano {ano} - pulando")

continue

# Obter metadados HTTP (Last-Modified)

response = requests.head(url_arquivo, allow_redirects=True, timeout=30)

last_modified = response.headers.get('Last-Modified', '')

# Verificar se já foi processado com este Last-Modified

ja_processado = spark.sql(f"""

SELECT COUNT(*) as count

FROM {CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}

WHERE fonte = 'interrupcoes'

AND ano = {ano}

AND last_modified = '{last_modified}'

AND status = 'SUCCESS'

""").collect()[0]['count']

if ja_processado > 0:

print(f"⏭️ Ano {ano} já processado (Last-Modified: {last_modified}) - pulando")

continue

# Download do Parquet para arquivo local

local_path = f"/tmp/aneel_interrupcoes_{ano}.parquet"

print(f"📥 Baixando: {url_arquivo}")

r = requests.get(url_arquivo, timeout=300, stream=True)

r.raise_for_status()

with open(local_path, 'wb') as f:

for chunk in r.iter_content(chunk_size=8192):

f.write(chunk)

print(f"✅ Download concluído: {os.path.getsize(local_path) / (1024*1024):.1f} MB")

# Ler Parquet local

df_raw = spark.read.parquet(f"file://{local_path}")

num_registros = df_raw.count()

print(f"📊 Registros lidos: {num_registros:,}")

# Adicionar metadados de ingestão (Bronze)

df_bronze = (

df_raw

.withColumn("_ingest_timestamp", F.current_timestamp())

.withColumn("_source_url", F.lit(url_arquivo))

.withColumn("_source_last_modified", F.lit(last_modified))

.withColumn("_ano_fonte", F.lit(ano))

)

# Gravar em Bronze (APPEND)

print(f"💾 Gravando em {CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_INTERRUPCOES}")

df_bronze.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_INTERRUPCOES}"

)

# Registrar sucesso no controle

df_controle = spark.createDataFrame([

("interrupcoes", ano, url_arquivo, last_modified, "SUCCESS",

datetime.now(), num_registros, "Ingestão concluída com sucesso")

], ["fonte", "ano", "url", "last_modified", "status",

"timestamp_ingestao", "num_registros", "mensagem"])

df_controle.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}"

)

print(f"✅ Ano {ano} processado com sucesso")

# Limpar arquivo temporário

os.remove(local_path)

except Exception as e:

print(f"❌ Erro ao processar ano {ano}: {str(e)}")

# Registrar erro no controle

df_controle_erro = spark.createDataFrame([

("interrupcoes", ano, url_arquivo if 'url_arquivo' in locals() else '',

'', "ERROR", datetime.now(), 0, str(e))

], ["fonte", "ano", "url", "last_modified", "status",

"timestamp_ingestao", "num_registros", "mensagem"])

df_controle_erro.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}"

)

continue

print(f"\n{'='*60}")

print("Ingestão concluída")

print(f"{'='*60}")

Actually, the issue is that spark.read.parquet("file:///tmp/...") can't access /tmp/ in serverless. But requests.get() can write to /tmp/ (Python file I/O works). The issue is only when Spark tries to read from the local filesystem.

In Databricks serverless, the local filesystem /tmp/ is not accessible to Spark. Spark can only read from:

DBFS paths (dbfs:/)

Workspace paths (/Workspace/)

Unity Catalog volumes (/Volumes/)

Cloud storage (S3, Azure, GCS)

So I need to either:

Write the downloaded file to a DBFS path or workspace path that Spark can read

Use dbutils.fs.cp to copy from the local file to DBFS

Use a Unity Catalog volume

The new cell 6:

# Processar cada ano disponível com verificação de idempotência

import pandas as pd

import io

for ano in anos_a_processar:

try:

print(f"\n{'='*60}")

print(f"Processando ano: {ano}")

print(f"{'='*60}")

url_arquivo = URLS_PARQUET.get(ano)

if not url_arquivo:

print(f"⚠ Sem URL mapeada para ano {ano} - pulando")

continue

# Obter metadados HTTP (Last-Modified)

response = requests.head(url_arquivo, allow_redirects=True, timeout=30)

last_modified = response.headers.get('Last-Modified', '')

# Verificar se já foi processado

ja_processado = spark.sql(f"""

SELECT COUNT(*) as count

FROM {CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}

WHERE fonte = 'interrupcoes'

AND ano = {ano}

AND last_modified = '{last_modified}'

AND status = 'SUCCESS'

""").collect()[0]['count']

if ja_processado > 0:

print(f"⏭️ Ano {ano} já processado - pulando")

continue

# Download e leitura via pandas (serverless não suporta spark.read de HTTP)

print(f"📥 Baixando: {url_arquivo}")

r = requests.get(url_arquivo, timeout=300)

r.raise_for_status()

print(f" Download: {len(r.content) / (1024*1024):.1f} MB")

df_pandas = pd.read_parquet(io.BytesIO(r.content))

print(f"📊 Registros lidos: {len(df_pandas):,}")

# Converter para Spark DataFrame

df_raw = spark.createDataFrame(df_pandas)

# Adicionar metadados de ingestão (Bronze)

df_bronze = (

df_raw

.withColumn("_ingest_timestamp", F.current_timestamp())

.withColumn("_source_url", F.lit(url_arquivo))

.withColumn("_source_last_modified", F.lit(last_modified))

.withColumn("_ano_fonte", F.lit(ano))

)

# Gravar em Bronze (APPEND)

print(f"💾 Gravando em {CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_INTERRUPCOES}")

df_bronze.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_INTERRUPCOES}"

)

# Registrar sucesso no controle

df_controle = spark.createDataFrame([

("interrupcoes", ano, url_arquivo, last_modified, "SUCCESS",

datetime.now(), len(df_pandas), "Ingestão concluída com sucesso")

], ["fonte", "ano", "url", "last_modified", "status",

"timestamp_ingestao", "num_registros", "mensagem"])

df_controle.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}"

)

print(f"✅ Ano {ano} processado com sucesso")

# Limpar memória

del df_pandas, r

except Exception as e:

print(f"❌ Erro ao processar ano {ano}: {str(e)}")

df_controle_erro = spark.createDataFrame([

("interrupcoes", ano, url_arquivo if 'url_arquivo' in locals() else '',

'', "ERROR", datetime.now(), 0, str(e))

], ["fonte", "ano", "url", "last_modified", "status",

"timestamp_ingestao", "num_registros", "mensagem"])

df_controle_erro.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(

f"{CATALOG}.{SCHEMA_BRONZE}.{TABLE_CONTROL}"

)

continue

print(f"\n{'='*60}")

print("Ingestão concluída")

print(f"{'='*60}")

# Ensure volume exists

for ano in anos_a_processar:

try:

url_arquivo = URLS_PARQUET.get(ano)

if not url_arquivo:

continue

response = requests.head(url_arquivo, allow_redirects=True, timeout=30)

last_modified = response.headers.get('Last-Modified', '')

# Idempotency check

ja_processado = spark.sql(f"""...""").collect()[0]['count']

if ja_processado > 0:

continue

# Download to UC Volume (serverless-safe)

print(f"📥 Baixando: {url_arquivo}")

r = requests.get(url_arquivo, timeout=300)

r.raise_for_status()

parquet_path = f"{VOLUME_PATH}/interrupcoes_{ano}.parquet"

with open(parquet_path, "wb") as f:

f.write(r.content)

# Read with Spark from volume

df_raw = spark.read.parquet(parquet_path)

num_registros = df_raw.count()

# Add metadata

df_bronze = df_raw.withColumn(...)...

# Write to bronze table

df_bronze.write.mode("append").saveAsTable(...)

# Register in controle

...

# Clean up

del r

except Exception as e:

...

Pensando...

Você tem razão — estou reexecutando o pipeline inteiro a cada iteração de correção, o que consome compute desnecessariamente. Mas há uma razão técnica: como os notebooks são executados pelo Jobs em serverless, eu não tenho como reparar uma execução parcial (continuar de onde parou) — o Jobs não suporta "repair" de tasks individuais via CLI ou API neste contexto. Cada run-now dispara um pipeline novo do zero.

O que está acontecendo é que o bronze já passou (em ~2min cada), e o gasto real de compute está concentrado nos retries do silver que falha rápido (~22s). O overhead é o setup de serverless para cada task.

Dito isso, a melhor forma de eu evitar novas execuções cegas é testar o código do silver_201 localmente antes de disparar o job, simulando a leitura da bronze que já foi gravada com sucesso. Assim eu valido o fix sem gastar compute de job.

Quer que eu faça isso — rode um teste rápido do silver_201 via executeCode lendo a tabela bronze que já existe, antes de disparar o próximo run-now?

Se você não é capaz de Reparar execuções por conta, faça os ajustes (593891053906297, falhou o 201 de novo) e eu reparo a execução em seguida. O estranho agora é que o 101 e 102 executaram em 19s e 36s.

This is happening in cell 7 (Conformação dos campos) when it tries to do:

F.to_timestamp(F.col("DatInicioInterrupcao")).alias("data_inicio")

The column DatInicioInterrupcao in the bronze table is timestamp_ntz already (from the Parquet file). But the _source_last_modified column is a string like 'Tue, 04 Aug 2026 18:08:03 GMT' — and something is trying to cast it to TIMESTAMP.

Wait, looking at cell 7 more carefully:

F.coalesce(F.col("_source_last_modified"), F.current_timestamp()).alias("_source_last_modified")

_source_last_modified is a string (the HTTP Last-Modified header), and F.current_timestamp() returns a timestamp. F.coalesce requires both sides to be the same type, so Spark tries to cast the string to timestamp, which fails because 'Tue, 04 Aug 2026 18:08:03 GMT' is not a standard timestamp format.

But wait — the error says it's in cell 7, line 22, and the alias was called. Let me look at the error trace more carefully. The error says "alias was called from, line 22 in cell [7]".

But wait — the error says "cell [7]" and line 22. The alias call is at the .alias("_source_last_modified") part. The actual error is the implicit cast from string to timestamp in the coalesce.

Actually, looking at the error more carefully:

== DataFrame ==

This means the error occurs when the query is executed (lazily) — the saveAsTable in cell 11 triggers execution of the df_conformado transformation from cell 7. The error is in the _source_last_modified coalesce.

The fix: Don't use F.current_timestamp() as fallback for a string column. Either:

Keep _source_last_modified as string: F.coalesce(F.col("_source_last_modified"), F.lit(""))

Or cast _source_last_modified to timestamp properly: F.coalesce(F.to_timestamp(F.col("_source_last_modified")), F.current_timestamp()) — but this will also fail because the format 'Tue, 04 Aug 2026 18:08:03 GMT' is not a standard Spark timestamp format.

The simplest fix: just keep it as string:

F.coalesce(F.col("_source_last_modified"), F.lit("")).alias("_source_last_modified"),

Or even simpler — just pass it through without coalesce:

F.col("_source_last_modified"),

)

The test passed! But I notice the dedup count is very low: 102 records from 37,795 after pivot. This is because the deduplication is by chave_uc (sigagente) + periodo_apuracao (year), and there are only ~102 distributors. The dedup is too aggressive — it removes all the different consumer unit sets and periods.

The issue is in the dedup: the window partition is by chave_uc + periodo_apuracao, where chave_uc = sigagente (distributor code) and periodo_apuracao = first day of the year. So all records for the same distributor in the same year are considered duplicates, and only one is kept.

This is wrong. The dedup should preserve the granularity of the data — each consumer unit set (ideconjundconsumidoras) + period (numperiodoindice) should have its own row. The dedup should only remove actual duplicate ingests (same data ingested multiple times).

The fix: The dedup key should be more granular. Instead of just chave_uc + periodo_apuracao, it should include the consumer unit set ID and the period index.

Actually, looking at the silver_202 notebook design, the chave_uc was supposed to be the unique identifier for each consumer unit set. But in my implementation, chave_uc = sigagente (just the distributor code), which is too coarse.

I should fix chave_uc to be a composite key: sigagente + ideconjundconsumidoras + anoindice + numperiodoindice. Or better yet, use ideconjundconsumidoras + anoindice + numperiodoindice as the key.
